Let me explain the defect chain before patching:\n\n**Root cause:** `DocumentIO.saveDocument()` decides whether to write to disk by comparing the editor text against the *in-memory* "initial version" (`Document.getInitialVersion()`), which is unreliable:\n\n1. Editor history is **disabled by default** (`AppSettings.isEditorHistoryEnabled()` → `false`), so the snapshot `DocumentEditFragment.loadDocument()` tries to take (`document.addToHistory()`) is a **no-op** — `_history` stays empty.\n2. After `HISTORY_DELTA` (5 s) the delayed runnable in `onContentEditValueChanged()` runs `_document.setContent(editorText)` — this is also what enables the save icon ("wait for the save icon to be enabled").\n3. Pressing save → `getInitialVersion()` sees an empty history, so it **lazily snapshots the current document** (which already contains the typed text) and returns it as the "initial" version.\n4. `!currentText.equals(documentInitial.getContent())` is therefore `false` → **the file is never written**, while `true` (success) is returned. All subsequent saves (onPause/back) compare against that same bogus `_history.get(0)` → never write.\n\nIf back is pressed within 5 s, `_document`\'s content is still `""` when the snapshot is taken → contents differ → file gets written. AssistantMessage(content=[TextBlock(text='Done. The project compiles (`BUILD SUCCESSFUL`) and the bug is fixed.\n\n## Diagnosis\n\nThe save button didn\'t just fail to save — **it actively poisoned every later save attempt**, including the one on back/`onPause` that normally creates the note.\n\n`DocumentIO.saveDocument()` decided whether to touch the disk by comparing the editor text against the *in-memory* "initial version": `!currentText.equals(documentInitial.getContent())`. That baseline is fabricated on demand:\n\n1. `AppSettings.isEditorHistoryEnabled()` defaults to **`false`**, so the snapshot `DocumentEditFragment.loadDocument()` takes via `document.addToHistory()` is a **no-op** (`addToHistory()` is gated on `_doHistory`) → `_history` stays empty.\n2. After `HISTORY_DELTA` (5 s) the delayed runnable in `onContentEditValueChanged()` runs `_document.setContent(editorText)`. This is also exactly what un-greys the save icon (`enable = !(content.isEmpty() || title.isEmpty())`) — i.e. *the icon only becomes usable once the state that breaks saving exists*.\n3. Pressing save → `Document.getInitialVersion()` finds an empty history and **lazily snapshots the current document**, which already holds the typed text, then returns it as `_history.get(0)`.\n4. `currentText.equals(documentInitial.getContent())` → `true` → no write, yet `true` (success) returned. Every subsequent save compares against that same bogus `_history.get(0)`, so back/`onPause` never writes either.\n\nPressing back within 5 s means `_document`\'s content is still `""` when the snapshot is taken → contents differ → file written. That\'s exactly the reporter\'s "works if I skip steps 3 and 4".\n\n## Patch\n\n**`DocumentIO.java`** — the write decision now consults the only authority on whether a write is needed, the file itself (`!exists() || disk content != text`), with the in-memory/history sync kept as a separate concern.\n\n**`DocumentEditFragment.java`** — the initial-version snapshot at load time is now taken regardless of the undo/redo preference (temporarily forcing `setDoHistory(true)`), restoring the invariant that `getInitialVersion()` reflects the state the document was *loaded* in rather than a mutated one.\n\n## Verification\n\nSince this logic is pure Java, I extracted `Document` plus the old/new save logic into a throwaway harness (removed afterwards) and replayed the reported gesture sequence:\n\n| Scenario | Before | After |\n|---|---|---|\n| type, **wait, press save**, back (bug repro) | **NOT CREATED** | CREATED |\n| type, back within 5 s (reporter\'s workaround) | CREATED | CREATED |\n| edit existing note, wait, save, back | **CHANGES LOST** | SAVED |\n| change title → rename on save | RENAMED ok | RENAMED ok |\n\nBoth preference states (`editorHistoryEnabled` true/false) pass after the patch, and the fix uncovered that the same defect silently **discarded edits to existing notes** — not only new-note creation.
